ParlaMint-AT_2011-03-31-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00100_d7e8347	Madam President. Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! In fact, it is gratifying today to deal with the fight against wage and social dumping. But where are the real problems, the real strains, the Austria and above all, the Austrian workers must experience thanks to your wrong decision or your inactivity on this question, Ladies and gentlemen of the government parties? - Right in a month, on the 1st. May, the Eastern opening of the European labour market is due to the fact that last year you were not prepared by SPÖ and ÖVP – despite the call of the Free Party – to take negotiations with the European Union in order to negotiate an extension. Soon we'll have the 1st. May and thus the opening of the Eastern European labour market. What does that mean? – This means that many unemployed people from there – in our Eastern European neighbouring countries Czech Republic, Slovakia, Hungary, there are a million unemployed people – can come to Austria on our labour market and get active here. The fact that this is, of course, a burden on the Austrian labour market should not be mentioned. And of course one must also mention that in these countries there are average incomes of net 350 € per month. If we make the lowest collective agreement in Austria, which is then legally paid out for those posted employees of foreign companies, then we get up at construction workers, at auxiliary workers in about €1200 net per month – and thus the Eastern European assistant worker deserves much more than home, where he earns an average of €350 per month, which of course represents a great incentive for Slovak construction workers, for Czech, for Hungarian construction workers, for auxiliary workers to come to Austria. We have a situation where we can say with fug and right that an Austrian skilled worker in this field has to earn between € 1,500 and €1 700, i.e. usually €500 more net, in order to feed his family here. Of course, there is the problem of the displacement process that will come to us. And this problem cannot be eroded by this law. People will come to us who, of course, are willing to work in the bottom collective agreement – and at home, in Slovakia, in the Czech Republic, in Hungary, with this lower collective contract content very, very well their family can be nourished. An Austrian worker, on the other hand, will not be able to do this and will be fully driven into an extortion process, and we will have to experience that this wage dumping, which results in a wage dumping – and that is a wage dumping – will affect many workers to become unemployed. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] We already have a high unemployment rate in Austria. Today, we have over 300,000 unemployed; up to 70 000 Austrians are in training programmes and fall out of unemployment statistics – and there are also people in Austria who are necessarily sent in early retirement. So, summa summarum, we have 500 000 people in Austria who are not at work. I expressly say this because these figures are always presented differently by you. When we calculate these three components together, this is about 500 000 Austrians, and we have a serious problem here. We also have the Red and White Red Card, which will also ensure that immigration to our country is also increased from outside the European Union, thus increasing the situation on the labour market. These are decisions that you have made by this Federal Government, which inevitably have to lead to a rise in the labour market, whereas we are free-traders. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] To a certain extent, this is an increase of Austrian workers, as you are dealing with this issue by SPÖ and ÖVP, because there is a big problem with us, which you are always trying to talk about. Time and again, you try to speak up. I can only emphasize: Today, with the cost of living in Austria, with the food costs, with the operating costs, with the cost of renting for a family, we have the situation that people in our country are becoming more and more under pressure; they can no longer get through with us today with a payment of the lower collective contract wage. In May, also for workers from the countries mentioned above. For example, it is made punishable for the breach of the basic wage. Basic wage means: Minimum gross wage according to collective agreement, overtime fees are taken into account – this also allows for quick action in this area, ladies and gentlemen – and a correct classification is to be taken into account. What are the penalties? – They move from € 1 000 to € 10 000 in the repeat case and more than € 2 000 to € 20 000 in the case of more than three workers concerned. The competent authorities are, on the one hand, an established competence centre at the Vienna regional health fund and the financial police, on the other hand, for employees insured in Austria, our local health insurance fund or in the construction sector, the building workers' holiday and handling fund. The criminal authority will be the county headquarters. What does the protection look like for our businesses? This is also an important point in this law. – Foreign companies must provide wage documents in German at the place of work in Austria. It is possible, for example, that there is a construction site check directly on site, in which services and bank documents must also be presented immediately. There will be a ban on the service if a penalty is made because the basic wage is lowered by more than three employees or repeatedly. The notification service is also possible at the site of use. The concept of safety performance should also be mentioned in this context. It is possible to access the labour wage if the suspicion exists of a basic wage breach or if a prosecution seems to be difficult or impossible. In other words, the client pays to the county captain. The second is the question of classification. This too is provided for in this law. It will no longer be possible – and we experience this again and again in practice – that auxiliary workers, that unqualified workers will be employed, but in reality they will have to do technical work and actually get a minimum skilled worker wage. There are also in the future – not yet! – better control. The risk has so far been, if you have been caught, that you have paid too little wages, that you have to pay down if necessary. That was the risk that an entrepreneur has had to this day. In the future, in addition to the repayment, there will also be the possibility of a penalty to be paid. In the most extreme case, the level of punishment goes up to € 50 000. And in certain cases, even if one does not comply with the provisions of the law to be adopted today, there is the possibility of a withdrawal of the commercial right. So very serious sanctions, which are theoretically in the law today and in practice can also become reality. A major challenge for the implementation of this law will be to resolve the question: How does the cooperation of the authorities work? On the one hand, we have the financial police or the KIAB, and on the other hand we have the organs of the local health phone. We also have the labour inspectorate that takes into account the health situation. And we have the criminal authorities. It will be a great challenge to organise cooperation between these authorities. I think it will also be necessary to make an evaluation of this law in the coming months, on-going and accompanying to see if it really is an instrument that helps in the positive sense, or whether re-adjustment is necessary. It is still important for me to point out that the criminality clearly applies only to the basic wage. But there is still a right to work in the labour market for allowances, Mrs Schatz! So, you can also demand all these things, even with regard to the requirement of social security contributions, only: You can't be punished! But we'll look at what that looks like. ParlaMint-AT_2011-03-31-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00100_d7e12035	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen of the House! After such an emotional debate about what we think is such an important law, the wage and social dumping law, I have something to do with it. If, in particular, you are blaming us with the fact that we did not agree to these transitional periods, I would like to add that these deadlines were part of the accession treaty of the new countries, which we very much agreed to in order to clarify. However, ladies and gentlemen, this wage and social dumping law also implements another point from the government programme and the forthcoming extension of the opening of the labour market, which, as has already been pointed out, has been carried out by 1. In May 2011, a rapid reaction was made. Especially in the context of the discussion on this subject, which was really very emotional and will also be conducted in the future, it must be mentioned that Austria as the only European country has not allowed these transitional periods and has maintained this 7-year transitional period for all employment groups. Germany, on the other hand, has put forward these transitional periods in advance for certain employment groups, i.e. before the end of this 7-year period. Practice has shown us that the different rules in the EU countries, as far as the level of remuneration is concerned, have, of course, allowed and led to uneven competition. The wage and social dumping law guarantees workers from the EU, third countries as well as the domestic labour force of the Austrian minimum wage for the first time and imposes penalties on under-paid workers. With the centres of competence, set up at the Wiener Landeskrankenkasse, the KIAB, the building workers' holiday and treatment fund, we have, I believe, important instruments that can combat wage and social dumping. Ladies and gentlemen, wage and social dumping is also a highly unsocial process, not only creates a cost-effective disadvantage for workers, but also an impoverishing impact on employees. The removal of taxes and social security contributions causes significant economic and economic damage. The advantages of wage and social dumping are unfair competition for me in the economy, of which the micro and small enterprises can sing a song because it makes it difficult to manage. The wage and social dumping law is an essential and important step towards a just and legally enforceable remuneration for all employees in our country. It also provides a good basis for fair competition in our economy. I am also confident that we will be able to deal with the challenges that the opening of the labour market will undoubtedly bring, and we will be able to keep our high level of employment on, and even expand. Estimated high house, wage and social dumping is not a cavalier's offence, should not be regarded as such and should in any case be fought and stopped. I believe we have created an appropriate legal basis with the wage and social dumping law, and we have set a good first step towards combating wage and social dumping. Thank you.

It's no longer bound to a season, it's no longer bound to certain sectors, and if someone comes as a seasonal egg, he or she gets an employment permit for six months, all tax charges are allowed to start, then once again can get a permit for six months, then he or she has to pause for a whole two months, and then the whole game starts from the front. [[Intrusions of the Abg. Hörl.]] Thus, for years and decades, someone can work here as a so-called seasonal – very rightless – and pay taxes and taxes, but he or she does not get rights. [[Abg. Hörl:... all rights, Mrs. Korun!]] This host worker model, this host worker policy, if I may remind you, is the basic evil, because it is the reason for the integration problems we now have – this host worker policy, which has been running past federal governments for decades, in which they have put their heads in the sand and said that these migrant workers are only guests here. It is a mystery to me how to be a guest in a country 30 years, 40 years, now 45 years, but this host worker model has been held. We have not put in place any integration measures, and today we are all wondering why many people who live in Austria have not been able to do well German for decades. Do not solve these problems with the extension of the host worker model, but continue these problems. That alone is reason enough why we will not vote in favour of Greens of this government proposal today. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] Second point: Unfortunately, no one has yet been interested in the qualifications of those migrant workers who are already living in Austria. Who is interested in the qualifications of family members when they come? Who is interested in the sometimes very good qualifications of recognised refugees who, after four years, five years of asylum procedures, have the right to stay here? Do you know what these people will do? – Mostly taxi-driving diploma engineers, taxi-driving doctors and doctors, because their qualifications are unfortunately not used here in this country. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Rosenkranz.]] These are just two examples to show how unforgivable and non-worldly the model you have presented is. The professional experience in Austria should be taken into account. That's nice and good. However, it is not nice and good that the professional experience of search professionals is only half taken into account, compared with the professional experience of particularly qualified workers – because professional experience in Austria should count. In addition, even after five years, people who are highly qualified do not have the security to stay here – after five years of work and tax figures in Austria. For comparison: If such a person immigrates to Canada, he can apply for Canadian citizenship after three years. [[Abg. May. Stefan: Exactly...!]] This is absolutely not a system with which Austria can compete to recruit well-qualified or highly qualified workers. This is actually a sham model with which you want to signal on the one hand: Yes, we need labour, we want to get them; but on the other hand, we need to build them. They are the obstacles so high that even highly qualified people will either not come, will not be able to come or will not come. Unfortunately, we cannot agree to such a non-world and naïve model of labour force immigration. I can only say: The direction would be correct in itself, but the execution is unfortunately anything but enough. Not enough, sit down!

ParlaMint-AT_2011-03-31-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00100_d7e13274	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that highly qualified people are already entering the country and will continue to do so, and integration is also possible where the will for integration is there. The Foreign Employment Act creates a maximum number of foreigners independent of the country, clear rules for the crediting of foreigners to the federal highest and, in accordance with a proposal from the social partners, a new system for the temporary admission of foreign seasonals. This regular-seasonal arrangement must be ensured in order to take into account workers who have already been active in liberalising the Eastern states as seasonal kidneys. These will, of course, have to be calculated from the total number of seasons when they migrate to other sectors. Furthermore, this seasonal arrangement is very important in order to continue to cover work peaks in the individual sectors by maintaining a high-performance seasonal model. Foreign employment is, of course, only the first step for the Red and White Red Card, which is composed of two parts. The first part is the establishment, the second part is this access to the labour market. We are in the process of introducing a criteria-led immigration system. The first are particularly highly qualified, the second floor, so to speak, are skilled professionals in occupations, and then key forces come from third countries. There are also foreign students studying in Austria. I think this is very important, because especially if we teach these fellow citizens a certain know-how, we should also have the benefit of it in the economy. All three categories are common, and that is, as a criterion in the assessment, the German language, because language is ultimately the key to integration. The advantages of the red-and-white red card are brief: The increase in Austria's attractiveness for top international workers to do work here; a flexible system aimed at the labour market so that we can respond to needs; the involvement – as mentioned above – of foreign students who are trained by us. All of this leads to an increase in our economy. But I would also like to mention briefly the concerns raised in the previous debate about opening the labour market for the so-called EU-8, which have not yet been included. It is a fundamental right for all EU citizens to be able to work with accession throughout the European Union, and if a nation joins, so are the rights of citizens. But that doesn't have to be a one-way street. We should take advantage of opportunities and not be afraid. A survey among young people recently found that 61 percent believe that this opening up of markets also offers opportunities for Austrians in the labour markets in the neighbouring countries. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Hörl.]] Even 50 percent can imagine working in these countries. I think this is a very beautiful representation that shows that not all of the fear falls. In order to develop a stable economic location, there is also a need for decent labour market capacities. The new Foreign Employment Act is a good tool to regulate the labour market, to create transparent opportunities for migrant workers and to enable integration through the workplace. As the first part of the Red and White Red Card, we can now adopt this Foreign Employment Act. The second part is already fixed, and I hope we can decide here soon.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-03-31-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00100_d7e21084	Mr President. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In my speech, I would particularly like to address the five-party request concerning personal assistance and would also like to express my pleasure that it has succeeded in deciding on this five-party request because I am convinced that this request is a very, very important and valuable step towards a step closer to these federal rules for people with disabilities. With this request, the Federal Minister is indeed asked or asked to work with the countries to draw up proposals for a uniform federal regime, because it is precisely the moment that personal assistance is unfortunately, one must say, in the field of disability assistance is regulated in the countries, which is why there are very different conditions. It is also a very limited group of people who can actually benefit from this personal assistance in the leisure field. Unfortunately, we have no exact figures – they are not available to the Federation. And it is also true that personal assistance in the field of leisure is currently granted only to those who are of working age and also have a very high self-organisation. As I said, the regulations are very different across countries, and there are also different models for the assistants, i.e. there is the employer model and the model of the free staff. I think that with today's proposal and the determination of the Federal Government in the government programme to examine and seek a uniform federal regime, we will take a very, very good step in the right direction. There is also from the previous year a decision by the National Social Representatives Conference within the framework of a working group in the Ministry to actually strive for this federal uniform scheme. I know that yesterday there was a first session in this area, and I am convinced that we are on a good path. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, personal assistance is, as has been said, very different, but one finds itself everywhere with personal assistance: Personal assistance should give disabled people the opportunity to shape their lives according to their own needs. At the moment, personal work assistance is uniformly regulated, but personal assistance is intended to cover all areas of daily life, to support where people need assistance and support due to their disability. The objective is a self-determined equal life for people with disabilities – this is also the goal of the UN Convention for people with disabilities, which Austria has ratified. And this proposal is a step closer to this objective. Ladies and gentlemen, this may sound so simple, I would like to illustrate this uniform system by an example for all. For us who are not disabled, or for me, which I am not disabled, it is quite simple or easy to move from one state to another. A person with disabilities must first consider: Can I actually use personal assistance in the new federal state in which I want my new food point? You have to settle a lot of things in advance and then perhaps still face insurmountable obstacles. That is why I am convinced that it is important to find a uniform system in this area so that disabled people can not only live their lives on paper, but can actually be lived by them. I am very confident that this request will bring us a step further along this path, and from this point of view I wish the Federal Minister a great deal of strength and sustainability that there can be an agreement with the countries.

For me, it would be important in this context, also with the request that we maintain this direction and also implement a great deal in these areas. I hope that we do well to ensure that people with disabilities are included, for example in the National Action Plan. There are a lot of examples, best-practice examples of which people can be invited to what you can include in which projects. – Stop alms thinking in Austria! – Thank you for the attention.

Every investment expert advises you to keep at least 10 percent in gold. Now this is a certain problem, because for this 10 percent you won't get interest. The problem, of course, has also been recognized by the central banks around the world and has found a way out of this gold interest rate. Now we are with Switzerland, which you mentioned, Mr Ikrath. The Austrian National Bank has transferred the money to Switzerland, where there is a trading place. This is an interesting trading place, however, because more goes out of this trading venue than in. In fact, most stocks are no longer available there. Why? What else is stored there in the large safes? – There, debt notes are stored by those who have received this gold at interest rates, and on these notes we insist that we get it back at any time. That's how it works! Now I wonder where the point is. If you keep a gold reserve, you need it for the emergency. If I sell my reserve for a debt note now and hope I get it back in case of an emergency, do you think it works? Do you think that works when dollar and euro go down the stream? We hope that won't happen. However, in case it happens, these gold reserves are also there. They are there to have appropriate safeguards in the event of an emergency. But if we give these gold reserves – and this is a very, very large part – abroad and sell them against debt notes, do you believe that we have a security in Austria? Faith You really do? That would be just as if you were expecting a famine, huge food supplies, which then sell against a debt note, and hope that if the need breaks out, you will get it back from that person. That's an illusion! I would therefore really ask that more honesty be exercised here. I know you know that, Mr. Ikrath. You know this gold's gone. The Austrian National Bank even admits that this gold is no longer physically there, at least two thirds of it. That's why I'd ask you: Let's talk about it quite openly! Let's get this gold home as long as we get something for these bills! Let's get it home! And then, if a crisis really breaks out, we also have the security you promise us to do so. [[BZÖ's condolences.]] We're supposed to have 280 tons of gold on the paper anyway. That corresponds to €10 billion, and that's our security. By the way, this says the Oesterreichische Nationalbank. That means, what makes my security sense if we have nothing but a debt note? Is that a security? Then we can sell these 280 tons immediately and start meaningful things with it, because in the event of an emergency, a debt note will not help us. We have seen this in the financial crisis. That's why: If there is security, then no experiments with it, and don't accept debt notes! Let's get the gold home! Then, in the event of an emergency, we have a security and not a debt note that will not be worth anything. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2011-03-31-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00100_d7e27151	Madam President. Minister! High house! My present request concerning the amendment of the Federal Law on the Equal Treatment Commission and the Court of Justice has the intention of creating the more precise definition of equal treatment and discrimination. Mrs Schwentner has justified the rejection of my request in the Committee by bringing about the occasion of a Salzburg tailor who specifically sought a woman in his job invitation. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to give you another example, which confirms my request. There was a key businessman who was looking for a male employee for sale and assembly by post. It did not take long, as the equal treatment attorney sent the company information material and a letter indicating that the equal treatment law requires a gender neutral job tender. It then received a letter of reply from the trader to the equal treatment attorney's office, in which he stressed that this person who receives this post had to lift and load up to 300 kilograms of heavy items and that a woman had never applied for this post in the company's 25-year practice. The firm also asked the equal treatment attorney to present him with an appropriate wife as an applicant. What the Court of Justice then did is that it raised the questions to the company to send a further letter of information containing detailed information on the equal treatment law. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, this example is from the 2008/2009 report of the Court of Justice. It shows that there are quite a number of other cases in which the equal treatment law has an adverse and far-reaching effect. But not only here, but also in other areas of women's policy, we are currently going in a completely wrong direction. I am referring here to the salary disclosure and also the planned requirement of quotas on supervisory councils of close-to-state establishments. We have discussed the Social Report today. Terriblely, we have had to hear that especially older women and single women are most affected by poverty. And what are you doing here? – They put in place pseudo-measures that de facto do not really take. For me, this demand for quotas is such a difference with the left eye for security positions, as one knows from Norway. De facto, it's nothing but proportional thinking and post-chacher.

Amon: Both tried, no comparison!]] Madam President. High house! Today's application deals with the punishment for those who abuse children who torture children, especially children to death. Here, from our point of view, there is an imbalance in the criminal code. For example, the quealing of children with death succession is according to § 92 para. 3 with no more than ten years of imprisonment. In comparison, the level of punishment for other offences, such as robbery with death or rape with death succession, is as follows: Here a life-long imprisonment can be imposed. From our point of view, this is wrong: Child protection, the protection of our children must be the same here! Therefore, we also call for the corresponding adaptation to the criminal code, because we consider: [[OBJECTIVES]] Those who torment children must not be excused, those who torment children must not expect mercy either, for it is the worst if someone robs children of any chance or punishes them in this way. But we do not just want to act in this area. As BZÖ, we have developed ten points for better child protection – including prevention. I would like to point out some, for example, the extension of the current mother-child passport to a parent-youth passport. But we also want that there is a functioning federal youth aid law quickly. The Secretary of State has promised that by the end of March also those countries which have not yet given their consent will agree. It's now late March, but we haven't heard anything about this – we're going to stick to it. We continue with our parliamentary initiatives, because it cannot be done differently in the federal states when children are at risk. Nor can it be that this should ultimately fail in money, so that nothing is done for the protection of children and young people. However, we as BZÖ also want a general obligation to display in case of suspicion of abuse and abuse. We want no limitation of criminal acts against sexual integrity and self-determination or the freedom of minor victims. We want a doubling of the criminal framework. In general, we want tougher penalties and a more consistent execution of punishment. I believe that there will be a great deal of discussion on this, also in connection with this proposal, which we are just discussing at first reading, because it is about our children and we should all be responsible and seeing the future. Thank you.
